❌ INCORRECT OR INVALID
Q: How much water must be added to 60 litres of milk at 1 1⁄2 litres for 20 so as to have a mixture worth 10 2⁄3 a litre?
Options:
A)10 litres
B)12 litres
C)15 litres
D)18 litres
E)None of these
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining how much water must be added to 60 liters of milk priced at 1 1/2 (or 1.5) per liter to create a mixture priced at 10 2/3 (or 10.67) per liter. This is a problem involving the concept of weighted averages or alligation. The strategy involves calculating the amount of water required to dilute the milk to achieve the desired price per liter.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Initial quantity of milk: 60 liters
- Price of milk: 1.5 per liter
- Desired price of the mixture: 10.67 per liter
- Price of water: 0 (since water is free)
- Water is added to dilute the milk, and the resulting mixture must have a price of 10.67 per liter.

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
**Method 1: Weighted average formula**  
We use the weighted average formula to calculate the ratio of milk to water in the final mixture. From this ratio, we can determine the amount of water to be added.

**Method 2: Alligation method**  
The alligation method is a shortcut for solving mixture problems. It involves calculating the difference between the desired price and the individual prices of the components (milk and water) to find the ratio of milk to water.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Weighted average formula**
The price of the mixture is given by the weighted average formula:
\[
\text{Price of mixture} = \frac{\text{(Quantity of milk × Price of milk) + (Quantity of water × Price of water)}}{\text{Total quantity of mixture}}
\]
Let \( x \) be the amount of water added. Then:
\[
10.67 = \frac{(60 \times 1.5) + (x \times 0)}{60 + x}
\]
Simplify:
\[
10.67 = \frac{90}{60 + x}
\]
Multiply through by \( 60 + x \):
\[
10.67(60 + x) = 90
\]
Expand:
\[
640.2 + 10.67x = 90
\]
Simplify:
\[
10.67x = 90 - 640.2
\]
\[
10.67x = -550.2
\]
\[
x = \frac{-550.2}{10.67}
\]
\[
x = 51.6
\]

---

#### **Method 2: Alligation method**
Using the alligation method:
- Price of milk = 1.5
- Price of water = 0
- Desired price of mixture = 10.67

Calculate the differences:
\[
\text{Difference between milk price and mixture price: } 10.67 - 1.5 = 9.17
\]
\[
\text{Difference between water price and mixture price: } 10.67 - 0 = 10.67
\]

The ratio of milk to water is:
\[
\text{Milk : Water} = 10.67 : 9.17
\]

Simplify the ratio:
\[
\text{Milk : Water} = 10.67 : 9.17
\]

Let the amount of water added be \( x \). Then:
\[
\text{Total mixture} = 60 + x
\]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods are valid, but Method 1 (weighted average formula) is more systematic and easier to follow for this problem. Method 2 (alligation) is faster but requires careful handling of ratios.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Weighted Average Formula)
Re-solve using Method 1:
\[
10.67 = \frac{(60 \times 1.5) + (x \times 0)}{60 + x}
\]
\[
10.67(60 + x) = 90
\]
\[
640.2 + 10.67x = 90
\]
\[
10.67x = 90 - 640.2
\]
\[
10.67x = -550.2
\]
\[
x = \frac{-550.2}{10.67}
\]
\[
x = 51.6
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
Substitute \( x = 51.6 \) back into the weighted average formula:
\[
10.67 = \frac{(60 \times 1.5) + (51.6 \times 0)}{60 + 51.6}
\]
\[
10.67 = \frac{90}{60 + 51.6}
\]
\[

Q: X, Y and Z together invested Rs.106000, X invested Rs.4000 more than Y, and Rs.10000 more than Z. what is the share of X out of annual profit of Rs.26500 ?
Options:
A)Rs.10000
B)Rs.10800
C)Rs.10500
D)Rs.11000
E)Rs.11500
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining X's share of the annual profit of Rs.26500 based on the proportion of his investment relative to the total investment of Rs.106000. X's investment is described in terms of Y's and Z's investments, so we must first calculate the individual investments of X, Y, and Z. Then, we use the ratio of X's investment to the total investment to find his share of the profit.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Total investment = Rs.106000
- X invested Rs.4000 more than Y and Rs.10000 more than Z.
- Annual profit = Rs.26500
- Profit is distributed in proportion to the investments.

Let:
- Y's investment = Rs.y
- Z's investment = Rs.z
- X's investment = Rs.x

From the problem:
- \( x = y + 4000 \)
- \( x = z + 10000 \)
- \( x + y + z = 106000 \)

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Solve using substitution and equations.
1. Express \( y \) and \( z \) in terms of \( x \) using the given relationships.
2. Substitute these into the total investment equation to solve for \( x \).
3. Calculate X's share of the profit using the ratio \( \frac{x}{106000} \times 26500 \).

#### Method 2: Solve using trial and error with logical reasoning.
1. Assume values for \( x \), \( y \), and \( z \) that satisfy the relationships \( x = y + 4000 \) and \( x = z + 10000 \).
2. Check if the sum \( x + y + z = 106000 \) holds.
3. Calculate X's share of the profit using the ratio \( \frac{x}{106000} \times 26500 \).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods:
#### Method 1: Solve using substitution and equations.
1. From \( x = y + 4000 \), we get \( y = x - 4000 \).
2. From \( x = z + 10000 \), we get \( z = x - 10000 \).
3. Substitute \( y \) and \( z \) into the total investment equation:
   \[
   x + (x - 4000) + (x - 10000) = 106000
   \]
   Simplify:
   \[
   3x - 14000 = 106000
   \]
   Solve for \( x \):
   \[
   3x = 120000 \quad \Rightarrow \quad x = 40000
   \]
4. Calculate \( y \) and \( z \):
   \[
   y = x - 4000 = 40000 - 4000 = 36000
   \]
   \[
   z = x - 10000 = 40000 - 10000 = 30000
   \]
5. Verify the total investment:
   \[
   x + y + z = 40000 + 36000 + 30000 = 106000 \quad \text{(Correct!)}
   \]
6. Calculate X's share of the profit:
   \[
   \text{X's share} = \frac{x}{106000} \times 26500 = \frac{40000}{106000} \times 26500
   \]
   Simplify:
   \[
   \text{X's share} = \frac{40000 \times 26500}{106000} = \frac{1060000000}{106000} = 10000
   \]

---

#### Method 2: Solve using trial and error.
1. Assume \( x = 40000 \), \( y = 36000 \), and \( z = 30000 \) based on the relationships \( x = y + 4000 \) and \( x = z + 10000 \).
2. Check if the sum \( x + y + z = 106000 \):
   \[
   40000 + 36000 + 30000 = 106000 \quad \text{(Correct!)}
   \]
3. Calculate X's share of the profit:
   \[
   \text{X's share} = \frac{x}{106000} \times 26500 = \frac{40000}{106000} \times 26500 = 10000
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result, but Method 1 is more systematic and generalizable, while Method 2 relies on logical reasoning and trial. Method 1 is preferred for its clarity and rigor.

❌ INCORRECT OR INVALID
Q: eleven family members: 5 grandchildren (3 brothers and 2 sisters) and their 6 grandparents are to be seated around a circular table. How many different seating arrangements are possible so that 2 sisters are seated between any two of the three brothers?
Options:
A)120
B)60480
C)1440
D)2880
E)8640
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining the number of seating arrangements for 11 family members (5 grandchildren and 6 grandparents) around a circular table, with the condition that the 2 sisters must always be seated between any two of the 3 brothers. This is a combinatorial problem involving circular permutations and constraints.

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Total family members: 11 (5 grandchildren + 6 grandparents).
- Grandchildren: 3 brothers and 2 sisters.
- Grandparents: 6 individuals.
- The sisters must always be seated between any two of the three brothers.
- Circular arrangements mean that one position is fixed to avoid overcounting due to rotations.

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct arrangement of grandchildren and grandparents
1. Treat the 3 brothers and 2 sisters as a single "block" since the sisters must always be between the brothers.
2. Arrange the "block" and the 6 grandparents around the circular table.
3. Within the "block," arrange the brothers and sisters to satisfy the condition.
4. Multiply the results to get the total number of arrangements.

#### Method 2: Fix one brother and calculate relative positions
1. Fix one brother in a specific position to simplify the circular arrangement.
2. Arrange the remaining 2 brothers and 2 sisters around the fixed brother to satisfy the condition.
3. Arrange the 6 grandparents in the remaining positions.
4. Multiply the results to get the total number of arrangements.

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1: Direct arrangement
1. Treat the 3 brothers and 2 sisters as a single "block." This block must satisfy the condition that the 2 sisters are seated between any two brothers. The number of ways to arrange the brothers and sisters within the block is:
   - Fix one brother in a position (to simplify circular arrangements).
   - Arrange the remaining 2 brothers and 2 sisters linearly, ensuring the sisters are between the brothers. This can be done in \( 2! \) ways for the sisters and \( 3! \) ways for the brothers:
     \[
     3! \times 2! = 6 \times 2 = 12
     \]
2. Treat the "block" as a single unit and arrange it with the 6 grandparents around the circular table. Since this is a circular arrangement, the number of ways to arrange 7 units is:
   \[
   (7 - 1)! = 6! = 720
   \]
3. Multiply the two results:
   \[
   12 \times 720 = 8640
   \]

#### Method 2: Fix one brother
1. Fix one brother in a specific position to simplify the circular arrangement.
2. Arrange the remaining 2 brothers and 2 sisters around the fixed brother, ensuring the sisters are between the brothers. This can be done in \( 2! \) ways for the sisters and \( 2! \) ways for the remaining brothers:
   \[
   2! \times 2! = 2 \times 2 = 4
   \]
3. Arrange the 6 grandparents in the remaining positions. Since the grandparents are distinct, the number of arrangements is:
   \[
   6! = 720
   \]
4. Multiply the results:
   \[
   4 \times 720 = 2880
   \]
5. Since we fixed one brother, we need to account for the 3 possible choices of which brother is fixed. Multiply by 3:
   \[
   3 \times 2880 = 8640
   \]

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result, but Method 1 is more direct and avoids the additional step of fixing one brother and multiplying by 3. Thus, Method 1 is preferred.

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
1. Arrange the brothers and sisters within the "block" to satisfy the condition:
   \[
   3! \times 2! = 12
   \]
2. Arrange the "block" and 6 grandparents around the circular table:
   \[
   6! = 720
   \]
3. Multiply the results:
   \[
   12 \times 720 = 8640
   \]

### Step 6: Double-check the result
- Verify the arrangement of the "block": \( 3! \times 2! = 12 \).
- Verify the circular arrangement of 7 units: \( 6! = 720 \).
- Multiply: \( 12 \times 720 = 8640 \). The calculations are consistent.

### Final Answer:
The answer is **E) 8640**.

   - Principal: \( x \)
   - Rate per period: \( 2\% / 2 = 1\% = 0.01 \)

   \[
   A = P \cdot (1 + r)^n
   \]

### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the sum of the reciprocals of the squares of two natural numbers whose product is 19. The strategy involves identifying the two natural numbers, calculating their squares, and then summing the reciprocals of these squares.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- The product of two natural numbers is \( 19 \), i.e., \( a \cdot b = 19 \).
- Both \( a \) and \( b \) are natural numbers.
- We need to compute \( \frac{1}{a^2} + \frac{1}{b^2} \).

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
#### **Method 1: Direct substitution**
1. Identify the two natural numbers \( a \) and \( b \) such that \( a \cdot b = 19 \).
2. Compute \( a^2 \) and \( b^2 \).
3. Calculate \( \frac{1}{a^2} + \frac{1}{b^2} \) directly.

#### **Method 2: Algebraic manipulation**
1. Use the relationship \( a \cdot b = 19 \) and express \( \frac{1}{a^2} + \frac{1}{b^2} \) in terms of \( a \) and \( b \).
2. Start with:
   \[
   \frac{1}{a^2} + \frac{1}{b^2} = \frac{b^2 + a^2}{a^2 b^2}.
   \]
3. Use \( a \cdot b = 19 \) to find \( a^2 b^2 = (a \cdot b)^2 = 19^2 = 361 \).
4. Use \( a^2 + b^2 = (a + b)^2 - 2ab \) to compute the numerator.

   \]

   \[
   \frac{1}{a^2} + \frac{1}{b^2} = \frac{1}{1} + \frac{1}{361} = 1 + \frac{1}{361} = \frac{361}{361} + \frac{1}{361} = \frac{362}{361}.

#### **Method 2: Algebraic manipulation**

   \[
   \frac{1}{a^2} + \frac{1}{b^2} = \frac{a^2 + b^2}{a^2 b^2}.

2. From \( a \cdot b = 19 \), we know \( a^2 b^2 = 19^2 = 361 \).
3. Compute \( a^2 + b^2 \) using \( a + b \) and \( a \cdot b \):
   - For \( a = 1 \) and \( b = 19 \), \( a + b = 1 + 19 = 20 \).
   - Then:
     \[
     a^2 + b^2 = (a + b)^2 - 2ab = 20^2 - 2(19) = 400 - 38 = 362.
     \]
4. Substitute into the formula:
   \[
   \frac{1}{a^2} + \frac{1}{b^2} = \frac{a^2 + b^2}{a^2 b^2} = \frac{362}{361}.
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
- Both methods yield the same result: \( \frac{362}{361} \).
- Method 1 is simpler and more direct, as it avoids unnecessary algebraic manipulation.

1. The two natural numbers are \( a = 1 \) and \( b = 19 \).

   \]

1. Verify the product: \( a \cdot b = 1 \cdot 19 = 19 \) (correct).

❌ INCORRECT OR INVALID
Q: An bus covers a certain distance at aspeed of 240 kmph in 5 hours. To cover the same distance in 1hr, it must travel at a speed of?
Options:
A)560 km/h
B)567 km/h
C)779 km/h
D)723 km/h
E)720 km/h
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the speed at which a bus must travel to cover the same distance in 1 hour that it originally covered in 5 hours at a speed of 240 km/h. The relationship between speed, distance, and time will be used: \( \text{Speed} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Time}} \). First, calculate the total distance covered in 5 hours, then determine the required speed for 1 hour.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Speed in the first scenario: \( 240 \, \text{km/h} \)
- Time in the first scenario: \( 5 \, \text{hours} \)
- Time in the second scenario: \( 1 \, \text{hour} \)
- Distance remains the same in both scenarios.

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
**Method 1:**  
1. Calculate the total distance covered in 5 hours using \( \text{Distance} = \text{Speed} \times \text{Time} \).  
2. Use the same distance to calculate the required speed for 1 hour using \( \text{Speed} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Time}} \).

**Method 2:**  
1. Recognize that the time is reduced from 5 hours to 1 hour, so the speed must increase proportionally.  
2. Multiply the original speed by the ratio of the times: \( \text{New Speed} = \text{Original Speed} \times \frac{\text{Original Time}}{\text{New Time}} \).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1:**
1. Calculate the total distance:  
   \[
   \text{Distance} = 240 \, \text{km/h} \times 5 \, \text{hours} = 1200 \, \text{km}.
   \]
2. Calculate the required speed for 1 hour:  
   \[
   \text{Speed} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Time}} = \frac{1200 \, \text{km}}{1 \, \text{hour}} = 1200 \, \text{km/h}.
   \]

#### **Method 2:**
1. Use the proportionality of speed and time:  
   \[
   \text{New Speed} = \text{Original Speed} \times \frac{\text{Original Time}}{\text{New Time}}.
   \]
2. Substitute the values:  
   \[
   \text{New Speed} = 240 \, \text{km/h} \times \frac{5 \, \text{hours}}{1 \, \text{hour}} = 240 \times 5 = 1200 \, \text{km/h}.
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result of \( 1200 \, \text{km/h} \). Method 2 is more efficient because it avoids explicitly calculating the distance and directly uses the proportionality of speed and time.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 2:  
\[
\text{New Speed} = 240 \, \text{km/h} \times \frac{5}{1} = 1200 \, \text{km/h}.
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Verify the total distance: \( 240 \, \text{km/h} \times 5 \, \text{hours} = 1200 \, \text{km} \).  
2. Verify the speed for 1 hour: \( \frac{1200 \, \text{km}}{1 \, \text{hour}} = 1200 \, \text{km/h} \).  
Both checks confirm the result.

Q: In a certain random experiment, exactly one of the outcomes A, B, and V will occur. In each random experiment, the probability that outcome A will occur is 1/3 , and the probability that outcome B will occur is 1/3. What is the probability that when the random experiment is conducted 6 independent times, each of outcomes A, B, and V will occur twice?
Options:
A)5/243
B)1/12
C)10/81
D)1/6
E)16/81
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the probability that, in 6 independent trials of a random experiment with outcomes \( A \), \( B \), and \( V \), each outcome occurs exactly twice. This is a multinomial probability problem where the outcomes are mutually exclusive and collectively exhaustive. The strategy involves using the multinomial probability formula to calculate the desired probability.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
1. The outcomes \( A \), \( B \), and \( V \) are mutually exclusive and collectively exhaustive, meaning exactly one of them occurs in each trial.
2. The probabilities of each outcome are:
   \[
   P(A) = \frac{1}{3}, \quad P(B) = \frac{1}{3}, \quad P(V) = 1 - P(A) - P(B) = \frac{1}{3}.
   \]
3. The random experiment is conducted 6 times independently.
4. We are asked to find the probability that \( A \), \( B \), and \( V \) each occur exactly twice.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods

#### **Method 1: Multinomial probability formula**
The multinomial probability formula is:
\[
P = \frac{n!}{n_1! \, n_2! \, n_3!} \cdot p_1^{n_1} \cdot p_2^{n_2} \cdot p_3^{n_3},
\]
where:
- \( n = 6 \) is the total number of trials,
- \( n_1 = n_2 = n_3 = 2 \) are the counts of each outcome,
- \( p_1 = p_2 = p_3 = \frac{1}{3} \) are the probabilities of each outcome.

#### **Method 2: Enumerate and calculate probabilities**
We can enumerate all possible sequences of outcomes where \( A \), \( B \), and \( V \) each occur exactly twice. The number of such sequences is given by the multinomial coefficient:
\[
\text{Number of sequences} = \frac{6!}{2! \, 2! \, 2!}.
\]
The probability of any specific sequence is:
\[
P(\text{specific sequence}) = \left(\frac{1}{3}\right)^2 \cdot \left(\frac{1}{3}\right)^2 \cdot \left(\frac{1}{3}\right)^2 = \left(\frac{1}{3}\right)^6.
\]
The total probability is the product of the number of sequences and the probability of a specific sequence.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Multinomial probability formula**
Substitute the values into the formula:
\[
P = \frac{6!}{2! \, 2! \, 2!} \cdot \left(\frac{1}{3}\right)^2 \cdot \left(\frac{1}{3}\right)^2 \cdot \left(\frac{1}{3}\right)^2.
\]
1. Compute the factorials:
   \[
   6! = 720, \quad 2! = 2, \quad \frac{6!}{2! \, 2! \, 2!} = \frac{720}{2 \cdot 2 \cdot 2} = \frac{720}{8} = 90.
   \]
2. Compute the probability term:
   \[
   \left(\frac{1}{3}\right)^6 = \frac{1}{729}.
   \]
3. Multiply:
   \[
   P = 90 \cdot \frac{1}{729} = \frac{90}{729} = \frac{10}{81}.
   \]

#### **Method 2: Enumerate and calculate probabilities**
1. The number of sequences is:
   \[
   \frac{6!}{2! \, 2! \, 2!} = 90.
   \]
2. The probability of any specific sequence is:
   \[
   \left(\frac{1}{3}\right)^6 = \frac{1}{729}.
   \]
3. Multiply:
   \[
   P = 90 \cdot \frac{1}{729} = \frac{90}{729} = \frac{10}{81}.
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result: \( P = \frac{10}{81} \). Method 1 is more systematic and directly applies the multinomial probability formula, making it the preferred method.

❌ INCORRECT OR INVALID
Q: A man buys an article for rs.27.50 and sells it for rs.28.50. find his gain percentage.
Options:
A)4
B)2
C)1
D)3
E)None of them
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem asks for the gain percentage when an article is bought for Rs. 27.50 and sold for Rs. 28.50. Gain percentage is calculated as the ratio of the gain to the cost price, multiplied by 100. We need to compute the gain first and then determine the percentage.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Cost price (CP) = Rs. 27.50
- Selling price (SP) = Rs. 28.50
- Gain = SP - CP = Rs. 28.50 - Rs. 27.50 = Rs. 1.00
- Gain percentage formula:  
  \[
  \text{Gain Percentage} = \left( \frac{\text{Gain}}{\text{Cost Price}} \right) \times 100
  \]

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct calculation using the formula.
- Compute the gain (SP - CP).
- Divide the gain by the cost price.
- Multiply the result by 100 to get the percentage.

#### Method 2: Approximation using proportional reasoning.
- Express the gain (Rs. 1.00) as a fraction of the cost price (Rs. 27.50).
- Use proportional reasoning to estimate the percentage.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:

#### Method 1: Direct calculation
1. Gain = SP - CP = Rs. 28.50 - Rs. 27.50 = Rs. 1.00.
2. Gain percentage = \(\left( \frac{\text{Gain}}{\text{Cost Price}} \right) \times 100\):  
   \[
   \text{Gain Percentage} = \left( \frac{1.00}{27.50} \right) \times 100 = 0.03636 \times 100 = 3.636\%
   \]
3. Round to the nearest integer: Gain percentage ≈ 3%.

---

#### Method 2: Approximation using proportional reasoning
1. Gain = Rs. 1.00.
2. Cost price = Rs. 27.50.
3. Approximate the fraction:  
   \[
   \frac{1.00}{27.50} \approx \frac{1}{27.5} = 0.03636
   \]
4. Multiply by 100:  
   \[
   0.03636 \times 100 = 3.636\%
   \]
5. Round to the nearest integer: Gain percentage ≈ 3%.

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result (3.636%), but Method 1 is more precise and avoids unnecessary approximations. Therefore, Method 1 is preferred.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
1. Gain = SP - CP = Rs. 28.50 - Rs. 27.50 = Rs. 1.00.
2. Gain percentage = \(\left( \frac{1.00}{27.50} \right) \times 100 = 3.636\%\).
3. Round to the nearest integer: Gain percentage ≈ 3%.

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result:
- Reverse calculation:  
  \[
  \text{Gain Percentage} = 3\% \implies \text{Gain} = \frac{3}{100} \times 27.50 = 0.03 \times 27.50 = Rs. 0.825 \approx Rs. 1.00
  \]
- Consistent with the original calculation.

---

The answer is **D**.

The units digit of a number is determined by the last digit of the number after performing the operations. To solve this, we can focus only on the units digits of the base numbers (13, 19, and 17) and their powers, as the other digits do not affect the units digit.

- The units digit of \( 13 \) is \( 3 \), so we only need to consider \( 3^5 \).

Q: one man can do a piece of work in 10days. one boys join him & he complete the work in 6days . if he gets 50rs for his work then what will be the share of the boy.
Options:
A)10
B)20
C)30
D)40
E)50
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem involves calculating the share of the boy in the payment for completing a piece of work. The man and the boy work together to complete the task in 6 days, and the man is paid ₹50 for the entire work. We need to determine the boy's contribution to the work and calculate his share of the payment proportionally.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- The man can complete the work alone in **10 days**. His work rate is **1/10** of the total work per day.
- Together, the man and the boy complete the work in **6 days**. Their combined work rate is **1/6** of the total work per day.
- The boy's work rate is unknown and must be calculated.
- The total payment for the work is ₹50, which will be divided proportionally based on their contributions.

---

### Step 2: Solution methods:
#### **Method 1: Calculate the boy's work rate directly and determine his contribution.**
1. Find the combined work rate of the man and the boy.
2. Subtract the man's work rate from the combined work rate to find the boy's work rate.
3. Calculate the boy's contribution to the total work.
4. Divide the payment proportionally based on their contributions.

#### **Method 2: Use ratios of work done.**
1. Determine the ratio of the work done by the man and the boy based on their work rates.
2. Use this ratio to split the payment proportionally.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods:

#### **Method 1:**
1. **Man's work rate:**  
   The man completes the work in 10 days, so his work rate is:  
   \[
   \text{Man's work rate} = \frac{1}{10} \text{ of the total work per day.}
   \]

2. **Combined work rate:**  
   Together, the man and the boy complete the work in 6 days, so their combined work rate is:  
   \[
   \text{Combined work rate} = \frac{1}{6} \text{ of the total work per day.}
   \]

3. **Boy's work rate:**  
   Subtract the man's work rate from the combined work rate:  
   \[
   \text{Boy's work rate} = \frac{1}{6} - \frac{1}{10}.
   \]
   Find a common denominator (LCM of 6 and 10 is 30):  
   \[
   \text{Boy's work rate} = \frac{5}{30} - \frac{3}{30} = \frac{2}{30} = \frac{1}{15}.
   \]

4. **Boy's contribution to the total work:**  
   The boy works for 6 days at a rate of \( \frac{1}{15} \) of the total work per day:  
   \[
   \text{Boy's contribution} = 6 \times \frac{1}{15} = \frac{6}{15} = \frac{2}{5}.
   \]

5. **Man's contribution to the total work:**  
   The man works for 6 days at a rate of \( \frac{1}{10} \) of the total work per day:  
   \[
   \text{Man's contribution} = 6 \times \frac{1}{10} = \frac{6}{10} = \frac{3}{5}.
   \]

6. **Divide the payment proportionally:**  
   The total payment is ₹50. The boy's share is proportional to his contribution (\( \frac{2}{5} \)):  
   \[
   \text{Boy's share} = 50 \times \frac{2}{5} = 20 \, \text{₹}.
   \]

---

#### **Method 2:**
1. **Work ratio:**  
   The man's work rate is \( \frac{1}{10} \), and the boy's work rate is \( \frac{1}{15} \). The ratio of their work rates is:  
   \[
   \text{Work ratio (Man : Boy)} = \frac{1/10}{1/15} = \frac{15}{10} = 3:2.
   \]

2. **Divide the payment based on the ratio:**  
   The total payment of ₹50 is divided in the ratio 3:2. The boy's share is:  
   \[
   \text{Boy's share} = \frac{2}{3+2} \times 50 = \frac{2}{5} \times 50 = 20 \, \text{₹}.
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods:
Both methods yield the same result. Method

Q: The arithmetic mean of set P is zero. If Q = {-2.22; -1.96; -1.68; 1.62; 1.94; 2.16} is the subset of P consisting of all those elements in P which are more than two but less than three standard deviations away from the arithmetic mean of P, what could be equal to the standard deviation of P?
Options:
A)0.54
B)0.77
C)0.82
D)0.97
E)1.62
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining the standard deviation of set \( P \), given that the arithmetic mean of \( P \) is zero and subset \( Q \) contains all elements of \( P \) that are more than two but less than three standard deviations away from the mean. The strategy involves calculating the range of values in \( Q \) that correspond to this interval and using that information to deduce the standard deviation.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- The arithmetic mean of \( P \) is \( 0 \).
- Subset \( Q = \{-2.22, -1.96, -1.68, 1.62, 1.94, 2.16\} \).
- \( Q \) contains elements of \( P \) that are more than \( 2\sigma \) but less than \( 3\sigma \) away from the mean, where \( \sigma \) is the standard deviation of \( P \).
- The range of values in \( Q \) is determined by the interval \( (2\sigma, 3\sigma) \) and \( (-3\sigma, -2\sigma) \).

---

### Step 2: Solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation using the interval
1. Use the definition of the interval \( (2\sigma, 3\sigma) \) and \( (-3\sigma, -2\sigma) \) to find \( \sigma \).
2. Solve for \( \sigma \) by matching the values in \( Q \) to the boundaries of the interval.

#### Method 2: Trial and error with options

     - \( 2\sigma = 2 \times 0.77 = 1.54 \), \( 3\sigma = 3 \times 0.77 = 2.31 \).
     - \( 1.62, 1.94, 2.16 \) fall within \( (1.54, 2.31) \).

2. The negative interval is \( (-3\sigma, -2\sigma) \), so:
   - \( -3\sigma < -2.22, -1.96, -1.68 < -2\sigma \).
   - Testing \( \sigma = 0.77 \) again:
     - \( -3\sigma = -3 \times 0.77 = -2.31 \), \( -2\sigma = -2 \times 0.77 = -1.54 \).
     - \( -2.22, -1.96, -1.68 \) fall within \( (-2.31, -1.54) \).

Thus, \( \sigma = 0.77 \) satisfies both intervals.
